, the first terms
of the holomorphic part of
\underline{\Phi}^{\mathbb{P}^{2}}_{1/2}[u_{\rm D}^{n}/(2\Lambda)^{n}]
, with
u_{\rm D}
and
0\leq n\leq 4
. In the
N_{f}=0
limit,

t\,q_{\rm uv}^{1/4}\to i
, such that the non-vanishing terms match with those
in Table
2
, and thus with the
results on Donaldson invariants in the literature, for example

[
118
, Theorem 4.4]
,
[
24
, Table 2]
.

6.4
Contribution from
u_{i},i=1,2,3
for odd intersection form

Besides the contribution from
u=\infty
discussed in the previous
subsections,
\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{J}
can receive contributions from the other
three singularities
u\to u_{i}
,
i=1,2,3
. We will determine these
contributions in this Subsection for the integrands of Section

6.1
for
k_{m,1}=1/2
and
3/2
and
L

an odd lattice. The analysis is similar to
Section
5.4
, but with the indefinite theta series

\widehat{\Theta}_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{J}
replaced by
\widehat{G}_{\mu_{1}}\Theta_{L_{-},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{-}}
. The analysis shows that these cusps
only contribute if
\sigma\leq-7
. In particular, the
u
-plane
integral for
\mathbb{P}^{2}
is completely determined by the
contribution from
\infty
.

Contribution from
u_{1}

For this contribution, we need to determine the leading behavior of
\widehat{G}_{\mu}(\tau_{1},\bar{\tau}_{1},v_{1},\bar{v}_{1};k_{m,1})
for
\tau_{1}\to i\infty
. To this end, we need to be
careful with contributions from the non-holomorphic part of
\widehat{G}_{\mu}
, which can contribute to the holomorphic part since
v_{1}

approaches
-\tau_{1}/2
for this cusp. We set
\tilde{v}_{1}=v_{1}+\tau_{1}/2
, which remains finite near the cusp.
To follow the structure we found in Section
5.4
, we
define

\begin{split}&G_{\mu,1}(\tau_{1},\tilde{v}_{1};k_{m})=e^{2\pi i(v_{1}+\tau_{1}/4)k_{m,1}^{2}}\\
&\qquad\times\left(G_{\mu}(\tau_{1},\tilde{v}_{1}-\tau/2;k_{m})-\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}+\mu}(\mathrm{sgn}(n)-\mathrm{sgn}(n-k_{m}/2))e^{-4\pi iv_{1}k_{m}n}q^{-n^{2}+nk_{m}}\right),\end{split}

where the sum over
n
are holomorphic contributions arising from the
non-holomorphic part (
325
). We find for the leading terms of

G_{1,\mu}
in the
q_{1}
-expansion

\begin{split}G_{0,1}(\tau_{1},\tilde{v}_{1};1/2)&=-e^{-3\pi i\tilde{v}_{1}/2}\,q^{7/16}+\dots,\\
G_{1/2,1}(\tau_{1},\tilde{v}_{1};1/2)&=e^{3\pi i\tilde{v}_{1}/2}\,q^{7/16}+\dots,\\
G_{0,1}(\tau_{1},\tilde{v}_{1};3/2)&=\frac{q^{-1/16}}{e^{5\pi i\tilde{v}_{1}/2}(-1+e^{4\pi i\tilde{v}_{1}})}+\dots,\\
G_{1/2,1}(\tau_{1},\tilde{v}_{1};3/2)&=\frac{q^{-1/16}}{e^{3\pi i\tilde{v}_{1}/2}(-1+e^{4\pi i\tilde{v}_{1}})}+\dots.\end{split}

(355)

Moreover,
\Theta_{L_{-},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{-}}(\tau_{1},v_{1}{\boldsymbol{k}}_{m,-})=e^{-2\pi i(v_{1}+\tau_{1}/4){\boldsymbol{k}}_{m,-}^{2}}\Theta_{L_{-},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{-}}(\tau_{1},(v_{1}+\tau_{1}/2){\boldsymbol{k}}_{m,-})
. Thus
the contribution from
u_{1}
reads

\begin{split}\Phi_{{\boldsymbol{\mu}},1}^{J}&=\frac{m^{\frac{\sigma}{8}-2}}{4\,\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{\chi-\frac{3}{2}\sigma+2{\boldsymbol{k}}_{m}^{2}}}\left(\frac{4}{\Lambda}\right)^{\frac{3}{8}(2\chi+3\sigma)}\\
&\quad\times\lim_{Y\to\infty}\int_{-\frac{1}{2}+iY}^{\frac{3}{2}+iY}d\tau_{1}\,\left(\frac{du}{da}\right)^{\frac{\chi}{2}-3}\Delta_{\rm phys}^{\frac{\sigma}{8}+1}\left(C\,e^{-2\pi i(v_{1}+\tau_{1}/4)}\right)^{{\boldsymbol{k}}_{m}^{2}}\\
&\quad\times G_{\mu,1}(\tau_{1},v_{1}+\tau_{1}/2;k_{m,1})\,\Theta_{L_{-},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{-}}(\tau_{1},(v_{1}+\tau_{1}/2){\boldsymbol{k}}_{m,-}).\end{split}

(356)

From the
\tau_{1}
behavior of
du/da
,
\Delta_{\rm phys}
and
C

near
u_{1}
, it follows that

\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{J}
receives a contribution from
u_{1}
for

\sigma\leq-15
for
k_{m,1}=1/2
or
\leq-7
for
k_{m,1}=3/2
.

Contribution from
u_{2}

For the contributions from
u_{2}
, we determine the expansion of the
“dual” function

\widehat{F}_{\mu,2}(\tau_{2},\bar{\tau}_{2},\rho_{2},\bar{\rho}_{2})=-i(-i\tau_{2}/2)^{-1/2}\,e^{2\pi i\rho_{2}^{2}/\tau_{2}}\,\widehat{F}_{\mu}(-1/\tau_{2},-1/\bar{\tau}_{2},\rho_{2}/\tau_{2},\bar{\rho}_{2}/\bar{\tau}_{2}).

We find using the
transformation of the Appell-Lerch sum (
535
) that
\widehat{F}_{\mu,2}
equals

\begin{split}&\widehat{F}_{\mu,2}(\tau_{2},\bar{\tau}_{2},\rho_{2},\bar{\rho}_{2})=e^{\pi i\mu}\,q_{2}^{-1/16}w_{2}^{-1/2}\left(\,M(\tau_{2}/2,\rho_{2}-\mu+\tau_{2}/4,1/2)\right.\\
&\left.\quad+\frac{i}{2}R(\tau_{2}/2,\bar{\tau}_{2}/2,\rho_{2}-\mu+\tau_{2}/4-1/2,\bar{\rho}_{2}-\mu+\bar{\tau}_{2}/4-1/2)\right)\\
&=F_{2,\mu}(\tau_{2},\rho_{2})\\
&\quad-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\left(\mathrm{sgn}(n)-E((n+2b_{2})\sqrt{y_{2}})\right)\,(-1)^{2\mu n}q_{2}^{-n^{2}/4}w_{2}^{-n},\end{split}

(357)

with

\begin{split}F_{\mu,2}(\tau_{2},\rho_{2})&=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\vartheta_{2}(\tau_{2}/2)}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}+\frac{1}{2}}\frac{q_{2}^{n^{2}/4}}{1-(-1)^{2\mu}w_{2}\,q_{2}^{n/2}}.\end{split}

(358)

This function is anti-symmetric in
\rho_{2}
. By (
334
) and (
337
), this also equals

F_{\mu,2}(\tau_{2},3z_{2}/2)=G_{0}(\tau,z;3/2)+e^{2\pi i\mu}\,G_{1/2}(\tau,z;3/2)+D_{\mu,2}(\tau_{2},z_{2}),

(359)

where
D_{\mu,2}
are the dual of the functions
D_{\mu}
(
335
),

\begin{split}D_{0,2}(\tau,z)&=-\frac{i\,\eta(\tau/2)^{3}\,\vartheta_{1}(\tau/2,z)\,\vartheta_{3}(\tau/2,z/2)}{\vartheta_{4}(\tau/2,3z/2)\,\vartheta_{2}(\tau/2)\,\vartheta_{2}(\tau/2,z)\,\vartheta_{4}(\tau/2,z/2)},\\
D_{\frac{1}{2},2}(\tau,z)&=-\frac{i\,\eta(\tau/2)^{3}\,\vartheta_{1}(\tau/2,z)\,\vartheta_{4}(\tau/2,z/2)}{\vartheta_{3}(\tau/2,3z/2)\,\vartheta_{2}(\tau/2)\,\vartheta_{2}(\tau/2,z)\,\vartheta_{3}(\tau/2,z/2)}.\end{split}

(360)

To determine the leading term near
u_{2}
, we set
v_{2}=\tau_{2}/2+\tilde{v}_{2}
, and introduce

\begin{split}&\widehat{G}_{\mu,2}(\tau_{2},\tilde{v}_{2};k_{m,1})=-i(-i\tau_{2}/2)^{-1/2}e^{2\pi iv_{2}^{2}k_{m,1}^{2}}e^{2\pi i(-v_{2}+\tau_{2}/4)k_{m,1}^{2}}\\
&\qquad\times\widehat{G}_{\mu}(-1/\tau_{2},(\tilde{v}_{2}+\tau_{2}/2)/\tau_{2};k_{m,1}).\end{split}

(361)

Using the explicit expressions, we can determine the leading term of the
holomorphic part of
\widehat{G}_{\mu,2}
. We find

\begin{split}G_{0,2}(\tau_{2},\tilde{v}_{2};1/2)&=\frac{e^{\pi i\tilde{v}_{2}/2}}{2(-1+e^{\pi i\tilde{v}_{2}})}\,q^{-1/16}+\dots,\\
G_{1/2,2}(\tau_{2},\tilde{v}_{2};1/2)&=\frac{e^{\pi i\tilde{v}_{2}/2}}{2(1+e^{\pi i\tilde{v}_{2}})}\,q^{-1/16}+\dots,\\
G_{0,2}(\tau_{2},\tilde{v}_{2};3/2)&=\frac{e^{3\pi i\tilde{v}_{2}/2}}{-1+e^{\pi i\tilde{v}_{2}/2}-e^{2\pi i\tilde{v}_{2}/2}+e^{3\pi i\tilde{v}_{2}}}\,q^{-1/16}+\dots,\\
G_{1/2,2}(\tau_{2},\tilde{v}_{2};3/2)&=-\frac{e^{3\pi i\tilde{v}_{2}/2}}{1+e^{\pi i\tilde{v}_{2}/2}+e^{2\pi i\tilde{v}_{2}/2}+e^{3\pi i\tilde{v}_{2}}}\,q^{-1/16}+\dots.\end{split}

(362)

The contribution from
u_{2}
reads

\begin{split}\Phi_{{\boldsymbol{\mu}},2}^{J}&=\frac{m^{\frac{\sigma}{8}-2}}{4\,\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{\chi-\frac{3}{2}\sigma+2{\boldsymbol{k}}_{m}^{2}}}\left(\frac{4}{\Lambda}\right)^{\frac{3}{8}(2\chi+3\sigma)}2^{-b_{2}/2}e^{\pi i\sigma/4-B({\boldsymbol{k}}_{m,-},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{-})}\\
&\quad\times\lim_{Y\to\infty}\int_{-\frac{1}{2}+iY}^{\frac{3}{2}+iY}d\tau_{2}\,\left(\frac{du}{da}\right)_{2}^{\frac{\chi}{2}-3}\Delta_{\rm phys,2}^{\frac{\sigma}{8}+1}\left(C_{2}\,e^{2\pi i(v_{2}-\tau_{2}/4)}\right)^{{\boldsymbol{k}}_{m}^{2}}\\
&\quad\times G_{\mu,2}(\tau_{2},v_{2}-\tau_{2}/2;k_{m,1})\,\Theta_{L_{-},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{-}}(\tau_{2}/4,(v_{2}/2-\tau_{1}/4){\boldsymbol{k}}_{m,-}-{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{-}/2).\end{split}

(363)

Therefore the cusp at
u_{2}
only contributes for four-manifolds with

\sigma\leq-7
.

Contribution from
u_{3}

One can show similarly that the cusp at
u_{3}
only contributes for

\sigma\leq-7
.

6.5
Evaluation for a special period point
J
and even
intersection form

This subsection evaluates
\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{J}
for
X
spin and
{\boldsymbol{k}}_{m}

an almost complex structure. If
X
is a spin four-manifold,

w_{2}(X)=0
and
{\boldsymbol{k}}_{m}\in L
. Moreover, the lattice
L
is even, and since
b_{2}^{+}=1
, it can be brought to the
form

L=\mathbb{I}^{1,1}\oplus nL_{E_{8}},

(364)

where
\mathbb{I}^{1,1}
is the two-dimensional
lattice with quadratic form
{\scriptsize\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&1\\
1&0\end{array}\right)}
, and
L
is minus the
E_{8}
root
lattice. We choose for the period point

J=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(1,1,{\bf 0}),\qquad C=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(1,-1,{\bf 0})

(365)

where the first two components correspond to
\mathbb{I}^{1,1}\subset L
, and
{\bf 0}
is the
(b_{2}-2)
-dimensional 0-vector. The squares
of the positive and negative definite components of
{\boldsymbol{k}}\in L
are

{\boldsymbol{k}}_{+}^{2}=\frac{1}{2}(k_{1}+k_{2})^{2},\qquad{\boldsymbol{k}}_{-}^{2}=-\frac{1}{2}(k_{1}-k_{2})^{2}+{\boldsymbol{k}}_{n}^{2},

(366)

where
{\boldsymbol{k}}_{n}\in nL_{E_{8}}
. We set furthermore

\mu_{+}=\mu_{1}+\mu_{2},\qquad\mu_{-}=\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}.

(367)

As before
{\boldsymbol{\rho}}=v{\boldsymbol{k}}_{m}
. We introduce

\rho_{+}=\sqrt{2}B({\boldsymbol{\rho}},J),\qquad\rho_{+}=\sqrt{2}B({\boldsymbol{\rho}},C).

(368)

The sum over fluxes
\Psi_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{J}
now factors for this choice of
J
as

\Psi^{J}_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}(\tau,\bar{\tau},{\boldsymbol{\rho}},\bar{\boldsymbol{\rho}})=\Psi_{\mathbb{I},(\mu_{+},\mu_{-})}(\tau,\bar{\tau},\rho_{-},\rho_{+},\bar{\rho}_{+})\,\Theta_{nE_{8},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n}}(\tau,{\boldsymbol{\rho}}_{n}),

(369)

with

\begin{split}&\Psi_{\mathbb{I},(\mu_{+},\mu_{-})}(\tau,\bar{\tau},\rho_{-},\rho_{+},\bar{\rho}_{+})=\exp(-2\pi yb_{+}^{2})\sum_{{\boldsymbol{k}}\in\mathbb{I}^{1,1}+(\mu_{+},\mu_{-})}\partial_{\bar{\tau}}(\sqrt{2y}(k_{1}+k_{2}+b_{+}))\\
&\qquad\times q^{(k_{1}-k_{2})^{2}/2}\bar{q}^{(k_{1}+k_{2})^{2}/2}e^{2\pi i\rho_{-}(k_{1}-k_{2})-2\pi i\bar{\rho}_{+}(k_{1}+k_{2})}\end{split}

(370)

where
b_{+}=\mathrm{Im}(\rho)/y
, and

\Theta_{nE_{8},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n}}(\tau,{\boldsymbol{\rho}}_{n})=\sum_{{\boldsymbol{k}}_{n}\in nL_{E_{8}}+{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n}}q^{-{\boldsymbol{k}}_{n}^{2}}e^{-4\pi iB({\boldsymbol{\rho}}_{n},{\boldsymbol{k}}_{n})}.

(371)

Our aim is to determine suitable anti-derivatives for

\Psi_{\mathbb{I},(\mu_{+},\mu_{-})}
,

\partial_{\bar{\tau}}\widehat{G}_{\mathbb{I},(\mu_{+},\mu_{-})}(\tau,\bar{\tau},v,\bar{v};{\boldsymbol{k}}_{m})=\Psi_{\mathbb{I},(\mu_{+},\mu_{-})}(\tau,\bar{\tau},v{\boldsymbol{k}}_{m},\bar{v}{\boldsymbol{k}}_{m}).

(372)

To this end, we decompose
\Psi_{\mathbb{I},(\mu_{+},\mu_{-})}
further by setting

n_{\pm}=k_{1}\pm k_{2}
. Then,

\Psi_{\mathbb{I},(\mu_{+},\mu_{-})}(\tau,\bar{\tau},\rho_{-},\rho_{+},\bar{\rho}_{+})=\sum_{j=0,1}s_{\mu_{+}+j}(\tau,\bar{\tau},\rho_{+},\bar{\rho}_{+})\,t_{\mu_{-}+j}(\tau,\rho_{-}),

(373)

with

\begin{split}&s_{\nu}(\tau,\bar{\tau},\rho,\bar{\rho})=\exp(-2\pi yb_{+}^{2})\sum_{n\in 2\mathbb{Z}+\nu}\partial_{\bar{\tau}}(\sqrt{2y}(n+b))\bar{q}^{n^{2}/2}e^{-2\pi i\bar{\rho}n},\\
&t_{\nu}(\tau,\rho)=\sum_{n\in 2\mathbb{Z}+\nu}q^{n^{2}/2}e^{2\pi i\rho n}.\end{split}

(374)

We define furthermore,

\theta^{\pm}_{\nu}(\tau,\rho)=t_{\nu}(\tau,\rho)\pm t_{\nu+1}(\tau,\rho).

(375)

These can be expressed as

\begin{split}&\theta_{\nu}^{+}(\tau,z)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\vartheta_{3}(\tau,z),&\nu=0\mod\mathbb{Z},\\
\vartheta_{2}(\tau,z),&\nu=\frac{1}{2}\mod\mathbb{Z},\end{array}\right.\\
&\theta_{\nu}^{-}(\tau,z)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(-1)^{\nu}\vartheta_{4}(\tau,z),&\nu=0\mod\mathbb{Z},\\
-e^{\pi i\nu}\,\vartheta_{1}(\tau,z),&\nu=\frac{1}{2}\mod\mathbb{Z}.\end{array}\right.\end{split}

(376)

We define new functions
f_{\mu}^{\pm}
as linear combinations of the
s_{\nu}
,

\begin{split}&f^{\pm}_{\mu}(\tau,\bar{\tau},z,\bar{z})=s_{\nu}(\tau,\bar{\tau},\rho,\bar{\rho})\pm s_{\nu+1}(\tau,\bar{\tau},\rho,\bar{\rho}).\end{split}

(377)

These are simply related to the functions
f_{\mu}
(
319
) from the discussion for odd lattices . We have

\begin{split}&f^{+}_{\mu}(\tau,\bar{\tau},z,\bar{z})=f_{\mu}(\tau/2,\bar{\tau}/2,z/2,\bar{z}/2),\\
&f^{-}_{\mu}(\tau,\bar{\tau},z,\bar{z})=e^{\pi i\mu}\,f_{\mu}(\tau/2,\bar{\tau}/2,(z+1/2)/2,(\bar{z}+1/2)/2).\end{split}

(378)

We arrive finally at,

\begin{split}&\Psi_{\mathbb{I},(\mu_{+},\mu_{-})}(\tau,\bar{\tau},\rho_{-},\rho_{+},\bar{\rho}_{+})=\tfrac{1}{2}f^{+}_{\mu_{+}}(\tau/2,\bar{\tau}/2,\rho_{+}/2,\bar{\rho}_{+}/2)\,\theta^{+}_{\mu_{-}}(\tau,\rho_{-})\\
&\qquad+\tfrac{1}{2}\,f^{-}_{\mu_{+}}(\tau/2,\bar{\tau}/2,(\rho_{+}+1/2)/2,(\bar{\rho}_{+}+1/2)/2)\,\theta^{-}_{\mu_{-}}(\tau,\rho_{-}).\end{split}

(379)

We proceed with
X
such that
b_{2}=2
, and
{\boldsymbol{k}}_{m}
being an almost complex
structure implies that
k_{m,1}=k_{m,2}=\pm 1
; we take
+1
without lack
of generality.
We have then
\rho_{+}=2v
and
\rho_{-}=0
. Therefore
\theta^{-}_{\nu}(\tau,\rho_{-})
vanishes, and it is not
necessary to find an anti-derivative for
f^{-}_{1/2}
.

We will determine a set of anti-derivatives for
\Psi_{\mathbb{I},((\mu_{+},\mu_{-}))}
in terms of the
function
\widehat{M}
(
531
), adapted for the present
context. We choose the arguments of
\widehat{M}
in such a way that
the theta series
\theta^{\pm}_{\nu}
in (
379
) cancel against the
theta series in front of the sum of
\widehat{M}
(
531
). To this
end, we let
\partial_{\bar{\tau}}\widehat{A}_{(\mu_{+},\mu_{-})}^{\pm}(\tau,\bar{\tau},z,\bar{z})=f^{\pm}_{\mu_{+}}(\tau,\bar{\tau},z,\bar{z})\,\theta^{\pm}_{\mu_{-}}(\tau,0)
, and define
\widehat{A}_{(\mu_{+},\mu_{-})}^{\pm}
as,

\begin{split}&\widehat{A}^{+}_{(\mu_{+},\mu_{-})}(\tau,\bar{\tau},z,\bar{z})=e^{-2\pi i(\mu_{+}-1/2)z}q^{-(\mu_{+}-1/2)^{2}/2}\,\theta^{+}_{\mu_{-}}(\tau,0)\\
&\qquad\qquad\times\widehat{M}(\tau,\bar{\tau},z,\bar{z},(1/2-\mu_{+})\tau+1/2,(1/2-\mu_{+})\bar{\tau}+1/2),\\
&\widehat{A}^{-}_{(\mu_{+},\mu_{-})}(\tau,\bar{\tau},z,\bar{z})=ie^{-2\pi i(\mu_{+}-1/2)z}q^{-(\mu_{+}-1/2)^{2}/2}\,\theta^{-}_{\mu_{-}}(\tau,0)\\
&\qquad\qquad\times\,\widehat{M}(\tau,\bar{\tau},z,\bar{z},(1/2-\mu_{+})\tau,(1/2-\mu_{+})\bar{\tau}).\end{split}

(380)

For the second line,
\mu_{-}\neq\pm\frac{1}{2}
, since
\theta^{-}_{\pm 1/2}(\tau,0)=0
. For the four choices of
\mu_{1,2}\in\{0,\frac{1}{2}\}
,
we arrive then at the following holomorphic parts

\begin{split}&A^{+}_{(0,0)}(\tau,z)=A^{+}_{(1,0)}(\tau,z)=\frac{1}{2}\vartheta_{3}(\tau)-\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{q^{n^{2}/2}}{1-e^{2\pi iz}q^{n}},\\
&A^{-}_{(0,0)}(\tau,z)=-A^{-}_{(1,0)}(\tau,z)=\frac{1}{2}\vartheta_{4}(\tau)-\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{(-1)^{n}q^{n^{2}/2}}{1-e^{2\pi iz}q^{n}},\\
&A^{+}_{(1/2,\pm 1/2)}(\tau,z)=-e^{\pi iz}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{q^{n(n+1)/2}}{1-e^{2\pi iz}q^{n}}.\end{split}

(381)

Using these functions, we can determine suitable anti-derivatives of

\Psi_{\mathbb{I},(\mu_{+},\mu_{-})}
. Let us first consider

(\mu_{+},\mu_{-})=(0,0)
. Equation (
379
) demonstrates
that the holomorphic part of a potential anti-derivative is

\frac{1}{2}A^{+}_{(0,0)}(\tau,\rho_{+})+\frac{1}{2}A^{-}_{(0,0)}(\tau,\rho_{+})=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{n\,\,{\rm even}}q^{n^{2}/2}-\sum_{n\,\,{\rm even}}\frac{q^{n^{2}/2}}{1-e^{2\pi i\rho_{+}}q^{n}}.

(382)

Substitution of
\rho_{+}=2v
, we find that this function has poles at

e^{4\pi iv}q^{2n}=1
for
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. Among these poles, the
ones at
e^{2\pi iv}q^{n}=1
are not excluded by (
69
). We
will substract these by subtracting the
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
modular
form,
i\eta(\tau)^{3}/\vartheta_{1}(\tau,z)
(
522
). The
holomorphic part of the anti-derivative for
{\boldsymbol{k}}_{m}=(1,1)
is therefore

G_{\mathbb{I},(0,0)}(\tau,v;{\boldsymbol{k}}_{m})=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{n\,\,{\rm even}}q^{n^{2}/2}-\sum_{n\,\,{\rm even}}\frac{q^{n^{2}/2}}{1-e^{4\pi iv}q^{n}}-\frac{i\eta(\tau)^{3}}{\vartheta_{1}(\tau,2v)}.

(383)

For
(\mu_{+},\mu_{-})=(\frac{1}{2},\pm\frac{1}{2})
, we arrive using a
similar analysis at

\begin{split}G_{\mathbb{I},(\frac{1}{2},\pm\frac{1}{2})}(\tau,v;{\boldsymbol{k}}_{m})&=\frac{1}{2}A^{+}_{(1/2,1/2)}(\tau,2v)+\frac{i}{2}\frac{\eta(\tau)^{3}}{\vartheta_{1}(\tau,2v)}\\
&=-e^{2\pi iv}\sum_{n\,\,{\rm odd}}\frac{q^{n(n+1)/2}}{1-e^{4\pi iv}q^{n}}.\end{split}

(384)

Finally, for
(\mu_{+},\mu_{-})=(1,0)
,

\begin{split}G_{\mathbb{I},(1,0)}(\tau,v;{\boldsymbol{k}}_{m})&=\frac{1}{2}A^{+}_{(1,0)}(\tau,2v)+\frac{1}{2}A^{-}_{(1,0)}(\tau,2v)\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{n\,\,{\rm odd}}q^{n^{2}/2}-\sum_{n\,\,{\rm odd}}\frac{q^{n^{2}/2}}{1-e^{4\pi iv}q^{n}}.\end{split}

(385)

These functions are closely related to the refined VW partition function
for
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}

[
119
, (4.36)]
. We comment more
on this relation in Section
6.6
.

Similarly to the case of odd intersection form, we define

g_{\mathbb{I},(\mu_{+},\mu_{-})}(\tau)=-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\partial_{z}G_{\mathbb{I},(\mu_{+},\mu_{-})}(\tau,z;{\boldsymbol{k}}_{m})|_{z=0},

(386)

and similarly for the non-holomorphic
\widehat{g}_{\mathbb{I},(\mu_{+},\mu_{-})}
.
This gives explicitly

\begin{split}&g_{\mathbb{I},(0,0)}(\tau)=-\sum_{n\,\,{\rm even}}\frac{q^{n(n+1)/2}}{(1+q^{n/2})^{2}}+\sum_{n\,\,{\rm odd}}\frac{q^{n(n+1)/2}(1+q^{n})}{(1-q^{n})^{2}},\\
&g_{\mathbb{I},(\frac{1}{2},\pm\frac{1}{2})}(\tau)=\sum_{n\,\,{\rm odd}}\frac{q^{n(n+1)/2}(1+q^{n})}{(1-q^{n})^{2}},\\
&g_{\mathbb{I},(\frac{1}{2},\pm\frac{1}{2})}(\tau)=2\sum_{n\,\,{\rm odd}}\frac{q^{n^{2}/2+n}}{(1-q^{n})^{2}}.\end{split}

(387)

Using these expressions, we can evaluate the
\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{J}
for all

b_{2}>2
, and
{\boldsymbol{k}}_{m}
of the form
{\boldsymbol{k}}_{m}=(1,1,{\boldsymbol{k}}_{n})\in(L/2)/L
, with
{\boldsymbol{k}}_{n}^{2}=-2n
such that
{\boldsymbol{k}}_{m}
is an almost complex structure.
Determination of
\Phi^{J}_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
then follows by taking the residue at

\tau-\tau_{\rm uv}=0
as in the case of odd lattices
(
343
). This gives our final formula
for
X
with an even intersection form
{\boldsymbol{k}}_{m}=(1,1,{\boldsymbol{k}}_{n})

\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{J}(\tau_{\rm uv},\bar{\tau}_{\rm uv})=\frac{\widehat{g}_{\mathbb{I},(\mu_{+},\mu_{-})}(\tau_{\rm uv})}{\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{2\chi}}\,\Theta_{nE_{8},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n}}(\tau_{\rm uv},0),

(388)

where

{\boldsymbol{\mu}}=(\frac{1}{2}(\mu_{+}+\mu_{-}),\frac{1}{2}(\mu_{+}-\mu_{-}),{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n})
. This
matches with known partition functions for VW theory if
X
is Fano.

6.6
Review of Vafa-Witten partition functions for rational surfaces

We recall a few aspects of partition functions of Vafa-Witten theory
[
15
]
for rational surfaces. Throughout this subsection,
\tau=\tau_{\rm uv}
and
X
one of the rational surfaces.
Let us introduce the Chern character
\gamma=(N,c_{1},k)
of a
U(N)

bundle with ’t Hooft flux (or first Chern class)
c_{1}=N{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
, and
instanton number
k
. The holomorphic part of the
U(N)
Vafa-Witten partitition function
with ’t Hooft flux
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\in\frac{1}{N}L/L
is a generating function
of weighted Euler characteristics reads

h^{X}_{N,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau)=\sum_{k}\bar{\chi}(\gamma)\,q^{k-\frac{N\chi(X)}{24}}.

(389)

On a Fano surface with
b_{2}^{+}=1
, the
\bar{\chi}(\gamma)
are
explicitly given by

\bar{\chi}(\gamma)=(-1)^{\mathrm{dim}_{\mathbb{C}}\mathcal{M}_{\gamma}}\sum_{m\geq 1,m|\gamma}(-1)^{\dim_{\mathbb{C}}\mathcal{M}_{\gamma/m}}\frac{\chi(\gamma/m)}{m^{2}},

(390)

where the
\chi(\gamma)\in\mathbb{Z}
are Euler numbers for
intersection cohomology
[
120
,
121
]
. Many explicit results for the generating series
h^{X}_{N,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}
are available for all
N

[
122
,
15
,
35
,
120
,
111
,
123
,
34
,
124
,
125
,
126
,
108
]
.

For manifolds with
b_{2}^{+}=1
, the VW partition functions are known to involve a non-holomorphic contribution
[
15
,
32
,
33
,
34
,
127
,
39
,
30
,
108
]
. With the appropriate non-holomorphic term, they transform as
[
15
]

\begin{split}&S:\quad\widehat{h}^{X}_{N,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(-1/\tau,-1/\bar{\tau})=(-1)^{(N-1)(\chi+\sigma)/4}N^{-\frac{b_{2}(X)}{2}}(-i\tau)^{-\frac{\chi(X)}{2}}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\sum_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}\in\frac{1}{N}L/L}e^{-2\pi iNB({\boldsymbol{\mu}},{\boldsymbol{\nu}})}\,\widehat{h}^{X}_{N,{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}(\tau,\bar{\tau}),\\
&T:\quad\widehat{h}^{X}_{N,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau+1,\bar{\tau}+1)=\exp\left(-\pi iN(N-1){\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{2}-2\pi iN\chi(X)/24\right)\,\widehat{h}^{X}_{N,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau,\bar{\tau}).\end{split}

(391)

We have furthermore
h^{X}_{N,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}=h^{X}_{N,-{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}=h^{X}_{N,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}+{\boldsymbol{k}}}
with
{\boldsymbol{k}}\in L
.

For
N=1
, we have the explicit result for any
X

[
122
,
15
]
,

h^{X}_{1,0}(\tau)=\frac{1}{\eta(\tau)^{\chi(X)}}.

(392)

In the following, we briefly review explicit expressions for
N=2
and
X=\mathbb{P}^{2}
and
X
an Hirzebruch surface.

The complex projective plane
\mathbb{P}^{2}

We fix
X=\mathbb{P}^{2}
and
N=2
and suppress these labels from the
generating functions. The holomorphic partition function
h^{\mathbb{P}^{2}}_{2,\mu}=h_{\mu}
takes the following
form
[
15
]

h_{\mu}(\tau)=\frac{g_{\mu}(\tau)}{\eta(\tau)^{6}},

(393)

Here,
g_{\mu}(\tau)
is the generating function of class numbers
H(4n-2\mu)
for
\mu=0,1/2
defined earlier in Equation (
340
), These are rational numbers for
\mu=0
. From (
390
), one can deduce that the generating series for the integer invariants is
\eta^{-6}
times

\begin{split}g_{0}(\tau)+\frac{1}{4}\,\vartheta_{4}(2\tau)^{3}=6\,q^{2}+2\,q^{3}+6\,q^{4}+12\,q^{6}+6\,q^{7}+\dots{}\end{split}

(394)

The functions
g_{\mu}(\tau)
are examples of mock modular forms, whose non-holomorphic completions read
[
15
,
37
]

\widehat{g}_{\mu}(\tau,\bar{\tau})=g_{\mu}(\tau)-\frac{3i}{4\sqrt{2}\pi}\int_{-\bar{\tau}}^{i\infty}\frac{\Theta_{\mu}(v)}{(-i(v+\tau))^{\frac{3}{2}}}dv,

(395)

with
\Theta_{\mu}
as in (
293
). The
g_{\mu}
transform as a modular vector under
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
. The transformations under the
S
and
T
generators are

\begin{split}&S:\quad\widehat{g}_{\mu}(-1/\tau,-1/\bar{\tau})=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(-i\tau)^{\frac{3}{2}}\left(\widehat{g}_{0}(\tau)+(-1)^{2\mu}\,\widehat{g}_{1/2}(\tau,\bar{\tau})\right),\\
&T:\quad\widehat{g}_{\mu}(\tau+1,\bar{\tau}+1)=(-i)^{2\mu}\,\widehat{g}_{\mu}(\tau,\bar{\tau}).\end{split}

(396)

The
U(2)
VW partition function for
\mathbb{P}^{2}
with ’t Hooft flux
\mu
is
[
15
,
30
]

\widehat{h}_{\mu}(\tau,\bar{\tau})=\frac{\widehat{g}_{2\mu}(\tau,\bar{\tau})}{\eta(\tau)^{6}}.

(397)

As mentioned in Section
6.2
, this is in perfect agreement with the holomorphic anomaly derived from the
\mathcal{N}=2^{*}

u
-plane integral (
291
), as well as the complete expression in Equation (
341
). The partition functions for the three gauge groups are

\begin{split}&\widehat{h}^{SU(2)}_{0}=\tfrac{1}{2}\widehat{h}_{0},\qquad\qquad\quad\widehat{h}^{SU(2)}_{1/2}=\tfrac{1}{2}h_{1/2}\\
&\widehat{h}^{SO(3)_{+}}_{0}=\widehat{h}_{0}+\widehat{h}_{1/2},\qquad\quad\widehat{h}^{SO(3)_{+}}_{1/2}=\widehat{h}_{0}-\widehat{h}_{1/2},\\
&\widehat{h}_{0}^{SO(3)_{-}}=\widehat{h}_{0}-i\,\widehat{h}_{1/2},\qquad\widehat{h}_{1/2}^{SO(3)_{-}}=\widehat{h}_{0}+i\,\widehat{h}_{1/2}.\end{split}

(398)

These functions satisfy the duality diagram in Figure
3
.

Let us also consider the refined partition function for
\mathbb{CP}^{2}

[
35
,
120
]
,

h_{\mu}(\tau,z)=-\frac{f_{2\mu}(\tau,z)}{\vartheta_{1}(\tau,z)^{2}},

(399)

which is the generating function of (intersection) Poincaré polynomials of moduli spaces of semi-stable sheaves. With
w=e^{2\pi iz}
, these are expressed in terms of the Appell-Lerch sum (
531
) as
[
116
]
:

\begin{split}f_{0}(\tau,z)&=\frac{1}{2}-q^{-\frac{1}{4}}w^{3}\,M(2\tau,4z-\tau,\tfrac{1}{2}-2z)\\
&=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{q^{-\frac{3}{4}}w^{5}}{\vartheta_{2}(2\tau,2z)}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{q^{n^{2}+n}w^{-2n}}{1-w^{4}q^{2n-1}},\\
f_{1}(\tau,z)&=-M(2\tau,4z-\tau,\tfrac{1}{2}-\tau-2z)\\
&=\frac{q^{-\frac{1}{4}}w^{3}}{\vartheta_{3}(2\tau,2z)}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{q^{n^{2}}w^{-2n}}{1-w^{4}q^{2n-1}}.\end{split}

(400)

They form a two-dimensional representation of
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
.
The functions
f_{j}(\tau,z)
are very closely related to the functions
G_{\mu}
(
334
).

G_{\mu}(\tau,z;3/2)=f_{2\mu}(\tau,-z/2).

(401)

The physical significance for VW theory of the structure of the poles as function of
z
of the
refined partition functions (
400
) is not well understood. Interestingly, the

\mathcal{N}=2^{*}

u
-plane integral gives some partial insight into this: The only allowed poles
are those given by (
69
). The poles of
G_{\mu}(\tau,z;3/2)
in
z
are thus not
“physical” singularities upon replacing
(\tau_{\rm uv},z)
by

(\tau,v/2)
.

The Hirzebruch surfaces

The holomorphic part of the
U(2)
VW partition functions can be evaluated for
all rational and ruled surfaces
[
35
,
111
]
. Using the theory of mock modular
forms, one can determine the non-holomorphic terms expected for
a partition function compatible with S-duality. Let
\Sigma_{n}
be a Hirzebruch surface, with self-intersection of the base curve equal to
n
, and canonical class
K_{n}
.
For a generic period point
J
, we express the partition function of
U(2)
VW theory with ’t Hooft flux
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
as

\widehat{h}_{2,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau,\bar{\tau};{\Sigma_{n}},J)=\frac{\widehat{g}_{2,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau,\bar{\tau};\Sigma_{n},J)}{\eta(\tau)^{8}}.

(402)

Then,
\widehat{g}_{2,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau,\bar{\tau};\Sigma_{n},J)
is given by
[
34
, Section
3.2]
17
17

17

The expression differs by a sign from
[
34
]
, since
[
34
]
defined generating functions in terms of “signed” Euler characteristics.

\begin{split}&\widehat{g}_{2,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau,\bar{\tau};\Sigma_{n},J)=g_{2,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau;\Sigma_{n},J)\\
&\qquad+\sum_{{\boldsymbol{k}}\in L+{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}\left(\frac{B(K_{n},J)\,|B({\boldsymbol{k}},J)|}{4\pi}\beta_{\frac{3}{2}}(4y{\boldsymbol{k}}_{+}^{2})\right.\\
&\qquad\left.-\frac{1}{2}\,B(K_{n},{\boldsymbol{k}}_{-})\,\mathrm{sgn}(B({\boldsymbol{k}},J))\,\beta_{\frac{1}{2}}(4y{\boldsymbol{k}}_{+}^{2})\right)q^{-{\boldsymbol{k}}^{2}},\end{split}

(403)

where

\beta_{\nu}(x)=\int_{x}^{\infty}u^{-\nu}e^{-\pi u}\,du.

(404)

If the period point
J
is chosen proportionally to
-K_{n}
, then
B(K_{n},{\boldsymbol{k}}_{-})
on the last line vanishes and the holomorphic anomaly is similar in form to (
395
). The non-holomorphic dependence in (
403
) matches with that found from the
\mathcal{N}=2^{*}

u
-plane integral (
291
).

7
Seiberg-Witten contributions for manifolds with
b_{2}^{+}>1

Besides the contribution from the
u
-plane
\Phi^{J}_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
, the partition function
receives in general localized contributions from the cusps
u_{j}
,

j=1,2,3
. These contributions are known as the Seiberg-Witten
contributions, and are a sum of (infra-red)
{\rm Spin}^{c}
structures
\mathfrak{s}_{\rm ir}
,

Z_{SW,j,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}^{J}=\sum_{\mathfrak{s}_{\rm ir}}Z^{J}_{SW,j,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\mathfrak{s}_{\rm ir}),

(405)

where
Z^{J}_{SW,j,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\mathfrak{s}_{\rm ir})
is the contribution from the cusp

u_{j}
for a fixed ’t Hooft flux
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
. The full partition function
thus reads

Z^{J}_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}=\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{J}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}Z_{SW,j,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}^{J}.

(406)

While
Z^{J}_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
depends on the metric through
J
for manifolds with
b_{2}^{+}(X)=1
, this dependence is absent for manifolds with
b_{2}^{+}>1
. In the latter case,
\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{J}=0
, and
J
can be omitted from the notation.

For
b_{2}^{+}=1
, the SW contributions
Z_{SW,j,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}^{J}
are not independent from the
u
-plane
contribution
\Phi^{J}_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
. Since the metric dependence of
Z_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{J}
can only come
from the non-compact region
u\to\infty
, wall-crossing of
\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{J}
at the
cusps
u_{j}
,
j=1,2,3
is cancelled by the wall-crossing of the

Z_{SW,j,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}^{J}
. Therefore, the change of
\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{J}
at cusp
j
across a wall equals

\left[\Phi^{J^{+}}_{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}-\Phi^{J^{-}}_{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}\right]_{j}=Z_{SW,j,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}^{J^{-}}-Z_{SW,j,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}^{J^{+}}.

(407)

The
u
-plane provides in this way a route to
determine the
Z_{SW,j,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}^{J}
for manifolds with
b_{2}^{+}=1
. Then using the topological nature of the partition function, this can be generalized to four-manifolds with
b_{2}^{+}>1

[
5
]
. In the remainder of this Section, we will determine the
Z_{SW,j,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}^{J}
by following this approach.

7.1
Seiberg-Witten invariants and wall-crossing

The Seiberg-Witten contribution from the cusp
u_{j}
is expressed as a residue in the local coordinate
a_{j}
. While
a
changes sign under the monodromy around
u=\infty
, the
local coordinate
a_{j}
near the cusp
u_{j}
is single-valued under
the monodromy. A key idea is that in the MQ formalism
a_{j}
should be identified with the first
Chern class of the Seiberg-Witten moduli space (for each component). The justification
is that it generates the
Q
-cohomology and has the right R-charge, which translates
into ghost number to be a degree two class. The SW moduli space is a union of projective spaces, and the contribution
Z^{J}_{{SW},j,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}
from each cusp takes the form

Z^{J}_{{SW},j,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}=\sum_{c_{\rm ir}}{\rm SW}(c_{\rm ir};J)\,\underset{a_{j}=0}{\mathrm{Res}}\!\left[a_{j}^{-1-n(c_{\rm ir})}e^{-S_{{SW},j}}\right],

(408)

where
{\rm SW}(c;J)
is the SW-invariant, and

n(c_{\rm ir})\in\mathbb{Z}
is half the real dimension of the SW moduli space (
130
). The term
e^{-S_{{SW},j}}
is the exponentiated action near the singularity, which is the product of the couplings
\mathcal{A}_{j},\dots,\mathcal{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\mu}},j}
and a normalization factor
\kappa_{j}
,

e^{-S_{{SW},j}}=\kappa_{j}\,\mathcal{A}_{j}^{\chi}\,\mathcal{B}_{j}^{\sigma}\,\mathcal{C}_{j}^{c_{\rm uv}^{2}}\,\mathcal{D}_{j}^{B(c_{\rm uv},c_{\rm ir})}\,\mathcal{E}_{j}^{c_{\rm ir}^{2}}\,\mathcal{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\mu}},j},

(409)

where
c_{\rm uv}=c_{1}(\mathfrak{s}_{\rm uv})
and
c_{\rm ir}=c_{1}(\mathfrak{s}_{\rm ir})
. The factor
\kappa_{j}=\kappa_{j}(\tau_{\rm uv},m,\Lambda)
is independent of
a_{j}
, but can in principle depend on
\tau_{\rm uv}
,
m
and
\Lambda
. On the other hand, the coupling
\mathcal{A}_{j}=\mathcal{A}_{j}(a_{j},\tau_{\rm uv},m,\Lambda)

can depend on
a_{j}
, and similarly for the couplings
\mathcal{B}_{j},\dots,\mathcal{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\mu}},j}
.
The coupling
\mathcal{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\mu}},j}
is a shorthand for the three couplings
depending on
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
,

\mathcal{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\mu}},j}=f_{1,j}^{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{2}}\,f_{2,j}^{B(c_{\rm uv},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})}\,f_{3,j}^{B(c_{\rm ir},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})}.

(410)

Since the lhs of (
413
) changes only by a phase under shifts of

c_{\rm uv/ir}
and
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
, the
f_{i,j}
are roots of unity,
independent of
a_{j}
,
\tau_{\rm uv}
,
m
and
\Lambda
. In total, we thus have eight couplings and a normalization to determine.

The wall-crossing of SW invariants cancels
the wall-crossing of the
u-
plane integral, such that the
wall-crossing of the full partition function is only due to

u=\infty
. The wall-crossing formula for Seiberg-Witten invariants
reads
[
128
,
64
,
129
]

{\rm SW}(c;J^{+})-{\rm SW}(c;J^{-})=\Delta{\rm SW}(c)=-(-1)^{n(c)}.

(411)

The rhs of (
407
) must therefore be of the form

\begin{split}\left[\Phi^{J^{+}}_{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}-\Phi^{J^{-}}_{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}\right]_{j}&=\kappa_{j}\sum_{c_{\rm ir}}(-1)^{n(c_{\rm ir})}\tfrac{1}{2}(\mathrm{sgn}(B(c_{\rm ir},J^{+}))-\mathrm{sgn}(B(c_{\rm ir},J^{-})))\\
&\times\underset{a_{j}=0}{\mathrm{Res}}\!\left[a_{j}^{-1-n(c_{\rm ir})}\,\mathcal{A}_{j}^{\chi}\,\mathcal{B}_{j}^{\sigma}\,\mathcal{C}_{j}^{c_{\rm uv}^{2}}\,\mathcal{D}_{j}^{B(c_{\rm uv},c_{\rm ir})}\,\mathcal{E}_{j}^{c_{\rm ir}^{2}}\,\mathcal{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\mu}},j}\right],\end{split}

(412)

where the precise sum over
c_{\rm ir}
depends on
j
, and follows
from the indefinite theta series (
307
), (
310
) and
(
312
). Substituting

\chi=4-\sigma
in (
413
) gives

\begin{split}\left[\Phi^{J^{+}}_{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}-\Phi^{J^{-}}_{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}\right]_{j}&=-\kappa_{j}\sum_{c_{\rm ir}}(-1)^{(c_{\rm ir}^{2}-\sigma)/8}\,\tfrac{1}{2}(\mathrm{sgn}(B(c_{\rm ir},J^{+}))-\mathrm{sgn}(B(c_{\rm ir},J^{-})))\\
&\times\underset{a_{j}=0}{\mathrm{Res}}\!\left[a_{j}^{-(c_{\rm ir}^{2}-\sigma)/8}\,\mathcal{A}_{j}^{4}\,(\mathcal{B}_{j}/\mathcal{A}_{j})^{\sigma}\,\mathcal{C}_{j}^{c_{\rm uv}^{2}}\,\mathcal{D}_{j}^{B(c_{\rm uv},c_{\rm ir})}\,\mathcal{E}_{j}^{c_{\rm ir}^{2}}\,\mathcal{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\mu}},j}\right],\end{split}

(413)

To determine the couplings from the
u
-plane integral, we have to
carefully compare (
408
) with wall-crossings determined in Section
5.4
.

A useful relation for the SW-invariants is

{\rm SW}(-c;J)=(-1)^{(\chi+\sigma)/4}\,{\rm SW}(c;J).

(414)

For the sums over
c_{\rm ir}
as in Equation (
413
), this implies the following identity

\begin{split}&\sum_{{\boldsymbol{x}}\in L}{\rm SW}(c_{\rm ir})\,\mathcal{D}^{B({\boldsymbol{x}},{\boldsymbol{y}})}\,\mathcal{E}^{{\boldsymbol{x}}^{2}}\\
&\qquad=(-1)^{(\chi+\sigma)/4}\,\mathcal{D}^{-B(c_{\rm uv},{\boldsymbol{y}})}\mathcal{E}^{2c_{\rm uv}^{2}}\sum_{{\boldsymbol{x}}\in L}{\rm SW}(c_{\rm ir})\,\mathcal{D}^{-B({\boldsymbol{x}},{\boldsymbol{y}})}\,\mathcal{E}^{2c_{\rm ir}^{2}-{\boldsymbol{x}}^{2}}.\end{split}

(415)

where
{\boldsymbol{x}}=c_{\rm ir}-c_{\rm uv}
and
{\boldsymbol{y}}\in H^{2}(X)
.

A four-manifold is said to be of
Seiberg-Witten simple type
if
{\rm SW}(c_{\rm ir};J)
is only non-vanishing for
c_{\rm ir}
, which correspond to an almost complex structure or equivalently
n(c_{\rm ir})=0
. While manifolds with
b_{2}^{+}=1
exihibit wall-crossing and are never of simple type, all known four-manifolds with
b_{2}^{+}>1
are of this type. An important example of a four-manifold of SW simple type is the K3 surface, whose only non-vanishing SW-invariant is
{\rm SW}(0)=1
. For later reference, we list the VW partition functions for K3
[
15
]

\begin{split}Z_{0}(\tau_{\rm uv})&=\frac{1}{4}\,G(2\tau_{\rm uv})+\frac{1}{2}\,G(\tau_{\rm uv}/2)+\frac{1}{2}\,G((\tau_{\rm uv}+1)/2),\\
Z_{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv})&=\frac{1}{2}\,G(\tau_{\rm uv}/2)+\frac{1}{2}\,G((\tau_{\rm uv}+1)/2),\qquad{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{2}\in\mathbb{Z},\,\,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\neq 0,\\
Z_{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv})&=\frac{1}{2}\,G(\tau_{\rm uv}/2)-\frac{1}{2}\,G((\tau_{\rm uv}+1)/2),\qquad{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{2}\in\mathbb{Z}+1/2,\end{split}

(416)

where
G(\tau)=\eta(\tau)^{-24}
. These partition functions are of the form (
405
) and (
406
) with
\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{J}=0
. Moreover, the term
\frac{1}{4}\,G(2\tau_{\rm uv})
for
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}=0
can be viewed as the
m=2
contribution to the rational invariant (
390
).

7.2
Determination of Seiberg-Witten contributions

In this subsection, we express the wall-crossing of
\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{J}
at

u_{j}
in a form similar to (
413
), which makes it straightforward
to read off the couplings
\mathcal{A}_{j},\dots,\mathcal{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\mu}},j}
. We determine the
\kappa_{j}
by comparison with the result for the K3 surface (
416
).

Contribution from
u_{1}

For
j=1
, we will find that the couplings read,

\begin{split}&\mathcal{A}_{1}=2^{1/4}\kappa_{1}^{-1/4}\,\Lambda^{-3/4}e^{\pi i/4}\frac{1}{\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})}\left(\frac{du}{da}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},\\
&\mathcal{B}_{1}=2\,\kappa_{1}^{-1/4}\,e^{3\pi i/8}\Lambda^{-9/8}m^{1/8}\,\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{\frac{3}{2}}\left(\frac{\Delta_{\rm phys}}{a_{1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{8}},\\
&\mathcal{C}_{1}=\frac{C^{1/4}}{\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{1/2}}\,e^{-\pi iv_{1}/2}\,q_{1}^{-1/16},\\
&\mathcal{D}_{1}=e^{-\pi iv_{1}/2}\,q_{1}^{-1/8},\\
&\mathcal{E}_{1}=e^{-\pi i/8}\,a_{1}^{1/8}q_{1}^{-1/16},\\
&\mathcal{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\mu}},1}=1.\end{split}

(417)

For fixed
c_{\rm ir}=4{\boldsymbol{\mu}}-2{\boldsymbol{k}}_{m}\mod 4L
with
\mathrm{sgn}(B(c_{\rm ir},J^{+}))=-\mathrm{sgn}(B(c_{\rm ir},J^{-}))>0
, Equation (
307
) shows that the wall-crossing at
u_{1}
is given by

\begin{split}\left[\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{J^{+}}-\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{J^{-}}\right]_{1,c_{\rm ir}}&=\frac{1}{64\pi i}\int_{-\frac{1}{2}+iY}^{\frac{3}{2}+iY}d\tau_{1}\,\frac{da}{d\tau_{1}}\,\left((2^{3/2}\Lambda^{-3/4}\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{-1}\left(du/da\right)^{1/2}\right)^{\chi}\\
&\times\left(2^{9/4}\,m^{1/8}\Lambda^{-9/8}\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{3/2}\,\Delta_{\rm phys}^{\frac{1}{8}}\right)^{\sigma}\\
&\times\left(C^{1/4}e^{-\pi i(v_{1}/2+\tau_{1}/8)}\,\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{-1/2}\right)^{c_{\rm uv}^{2}}\,\\
&\times\left(e^{-\pi iv_{1}/2}\,q_{1}^{-1/8}\right)^{B(c_{\rm ir},c_{\rm uv})}\,q_{1}^{-c_{\rm ir}^{2}/16},\end{split}

(418)

where we substituted (
566
) for
da/d\tau
. Next, we change
the integration variable to the local coordinate
a_{1}=2a-m
. Since

da/d\tau
(
566
) is a power series in
q^{1/2}
for
large
{\rm Im}(\tau)
, we learn that
a_{1}
is well-defined under the monodromy
M_{1}
. This is also
expected physically since a single hypermultiplet becomes
massless. Comparing with (
413
), we can read off the couplings

\mathcal{C}_{1}
,
\mathcal{D}_{1}
,
\mathcal{E}_{1}
and
\mathcal{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\mu}},1}
in agreement with (
417
), such that (
418
)
becomes

\begin{split}\left[\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{J^{+}}-\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{J^{-}}\right]_{1,c_{\rm ir}}&=\frac{1}{32}\,\underset{a_{1}=0}{\mathrm{Res}}\!\left[\left((2^{3/2}\Lambda^{-3/4}\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{-1}\left(du/da\right)^{1/2}\right)^{\chi}\right.\\
&\times\left(2^{9/4}\,m^{1/8}\Lambda^{-9/8}\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{3/2}\,\Delta_{\rm phys}^{\frac{1}{8}}\right)^{\sigma}\\
&\times\left.\mathcal{C}_{1}^{c_{\rm uv}^{2}}\,\mathcal{D}_{1}^{B(c_{\rm ir},c_{\rm uv})}\,\mathcal{E}_{1}^{c_{\rm ir}^{2}}\right].\end{split}

(419)

Substituting
\chi=4-\sigma
, we determine
\mathcal{A}_{1}
and
\mathcal{B}_{1}
in agreement with (
417
).

Using (
566
), we find for the behavior of the local coordinate
a_{1}
as function of
\tau_{1}

near the singularity,

a_{1}=16\,m\frac{\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{6}}{\vartheta_{2}(\tau_{\rm uv})^{6}}\,q_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}+O(q_{1}^{1}).

(420)

The leading constants in the
q_{1}
-expansion of the couplings are,

\begin{split}\mathcal{A}_{1}&=\,2^{5/4}\,\kappa_{1}^{-1/4}\,\Lambda^{-3/4}m^{\frac{1}{2}}\,e^{2\pi i/8}\,\frac{\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{2}}{\vartheta_{2}(\tau_{\rm uv})}+\dots,\\
&=2^{1/4}\Lambda^{-3/4}m^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\,e^{2\pi i/8}\,\frac{\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{3}}{\eta(2\tau_{\rm uv})^{2}}+\dots,\\
\mathcal{B}_{1}&=2^{5/4}\kappa_{1}^{-1/4}\,m^{\frac{3}{4}}\,e^{3\pi i/8}\Lambda^{-9/8}\left(\frac{\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{5}}{\vartheta_{2}(\tau_{\rm uv})}\right)^{3/4}+\dots,\\
&=2^{1/2}\,m^{\frac{3}{4}}\,e^{3\pi i/8}\Lambda^{-9/8}\left(\frac{\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{3}}{\eta(2\tau_{\rm uv})}\right)^{3/2}+\dots,\\
\mathcal{C}_{1}&=\left(\frac{\Lambda}{m}\right)^{\frac{3}{8}}\frac{1}{\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{1/2}\,\vartheta_{2}(\tau_{\rm uv})^{1/2}}+\dots,\\
&=2^{-1/2}\left(\frac{\Lambda}{m}\right)^{\frac{3}{8}}\frac{1}{\eta(2\tau_{\rm uv})}+\dots,\\
\mathcal{D}_{1}&=\left(\frac{\vartheta_{3}(2\tau_{\rm uv})}{\vartheta_{2}(2\tau_{\rm uv})}\right)^{1/2}+\dots,\\
\mathcal{E}_{1}&=2^{1/2}\,m^{1/8}e^{-\pi i/8}\left(\frac{\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})}{\vartheta_{2}(\tau_{\rm uv})}\right)^{3/4}+\dots\\
&=2^{-1/4}\,m^{1/8}e^{-\pi i/8}\left(\frac{\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})}{\eta(2\tau_{\rm uv})}\right)^{3/2}+\dots.\end{split}

(421)

where we used
\vartheta_{2}(\tau_{\rm uv})\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})=2\,\eta(2\tau_{\rm uv})^{2}
to simplify expressions.

For simplicity, we assume in the following that
X
has
b_{2}^{+}\geq 3
, and is of Seiberg-Witten simple
type. Then
J
can be omitted from the notation,
c_{\rm ir}^{2}=2\chi+3\sigma
, and the leading terms in the expansions (
421
) suffice. Combining all the terms, we find that the contribution from
u_{1}
is

\begin{split}Z_{SW,1,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv})&=\kappa_{1}^{1-(\chi+\sigma)/4}\,2^{-(\chi+\sigma)/4-c_{\rm uv}^{2}/2}\,\left(\frac{\Lambda}{m}\right)^{\frac{3}{8}(c_{\rm uv}^{2}-2\chi-3\sigma)}\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{6\chi+9\sigma}\eta(2\tau_{\rm uv})^{-5\chi-6\sigma-c_{\rm uv}^{2}}\\
&\quad\times\sum_{c_{\rm ir}=4{\boldsymbol{\mu}}-c_{\rm uv}\mod 4L}{\rm SW}(c_{\rm ir})\left(\frac{\vartheta_{3}(2\tau_{\rm uv})}{\vartheta_{2}(2\tau_{\rm uv})}\right)^{B(c_{\rm ir},c_{\rm uv})/2}.\end{split}

(422)

We can rewrite this in a more convenient form to compare to

[
15
,
16
]
. To this end, we replace
c_{\rm ir}
by
-c_{\rm ir}
in (
422
) and substitute (
414
) for

{\rm SW}(-c_{\rm ir};J)
. Since now
c_{1}\in-4{\boldsymbol{\mu}}+c_{\rm uv}\mod 4L=4{\boldsymbol{\mu}}+c_{\rm uv}\mod 4L
, we introduce
{\boldsymbol{x}}\in 2{\boldsymbol{\mu}}+2L

defined by

c_{\rm ir}=2{\boldsymbol{x}}+c_{\rm uv}=2{\boldsymbol{x}}+2{\boldsymbol{k}}_{m}.

(423)

Using moreover the identity
(
512
) and

\vartheta_{4}(2\tau_{\rm uv})=\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{2}/\eta(2\tau_{\rm uv})
, we write
Z_{SW,1,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}
as the sum,

Z_{SW,1,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv})=\sum_{{\boldsymbol{x}}=2{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\mod 2L}Z_{SW,1,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv};{\boldsymbol{x}}),

(424)

with summand,

\begin{split}Z_{SW,1,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv};{\boldsymbol{x}})&=\kappa_{1}\,\,{\rm SW}(c_{\rm ir})\left(-2\,\kappa_{1}\,\eta(2\tau_{\rm uv})^{12}\right)^{-\chi_{\rm h}}\,\left(\frac{(\Lambda/m)^{3}}{4\,\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{4}\,\vartheta_{3}(2\tau_{\rm uv})}\right)^{\ell}\\
&\quad\times\left(\frac{\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{2}}{\vartheta_{3}(2\tau_{\rm uv})}\right)^{\lambda}\left(\frac{\vartheta_{3}(2\tau_{\rm uv})}{\vartheta_{2}(2\tau_{\rm uv})}\right)^{{\boldsymbol{x}}^{2}},\end{split}

(425)

where we used
\ell
(
131
) to substitute for
c_{\rm uv}
, and also
\lambda
(
86
) and
\chi_{\rm h}
(
87
) to shorten notation.

Let us specialize to
X=K3
to fix
\kappa_{1}
. The only non-vanishing SW-invariant is for
c_{\rm ir}=0
. With

(\chi,\sigma)=(24,-16)
, we find for the contribution at
u_{1}
,

Z^{K3}_{SW,1,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{rl}\kappa_{1}^{-1}\,\left(2^{4}t^{3}\right)^{-\ell}\frac{1}{4\,\eta(2\tau_{\rm uv})^{24+8\ell}},&\qquad c_{\rm uv}=4{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\mod 4L,\\
0,&\qquad{\rm otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

(426)

We can compare this result for
c_{\rm uv}=0
with the Vafa-Witten result for
K3
(
416
). It is clear that we should compare (
426
) with
the first term in (
416
), which demonstrates that

\kappa_{1}=1
. Our final formula for the
u_{1}
contribution is then,

\boxed{\begin{split}Z_{SW,1,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv})&=\left(-2\,\eta(2\tau_{\rm uv})^{12}\right)^{-\chi_{\rm h}}\,\left(4\,t^{3}\,\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{4}\,\vartheta_{3}(2\tau_{\rm uv})^{4}\right)^{-\ell}\,\left(\frac{\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{2}}{\vartheta_{3}(2\tau_{\rm uv})}\right)^{\lambda}\\
&\quad\times\sum_{{\boldsymbol{x}}=2{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\mod 2L}{\rm SW}(c_{\rm ir})\left(\frac{\vartheta_{3}(2\tau_{\rm uv})}{\vartheta_{2}(2\tau_{\rm uv})}\right)^{{\boldsymbol{x}}^{2}}.\end{split}}

(427)

Contribution from
u_{2}

For the contribution from
u_{2}
, we proceed similarly by determining
the couplings
\mathcal{A}_{2},\dots,\mathcal{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\mu}},2}
from (
310
). The local
coordinate near
u_{2}
is
a_{2}=-2a_{D}+m
. The wall-crossing contribution from
u_{2}
reads

\begin{split}\left[\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{J}-\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{J^{\prime}}\right]_{2}&=\kappa_{2}\,(-1)^{n(c_{\rm ir})}\,\sum_{c_{\rm ir}=c_{\rm uv}\mod 2L}\tfrac{1}{2}(\mathrm{sgn}(B(c_{\rm ir},J))-\mathrm{sgn}(B(c_{\rm ir},J^{\prime})))\\
&\quad\times\,\underset{a_{2}=0}{\mathrm{Res}}\left[a_{2}^{-1-n(c_{\rm ir})}\mathcal{A}_{2}^{\chi}\,\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\sigma}\,\mathcal{C}_{2}^{c_{\rm uv}^{2}}\,\mathcal{D}_{2}^{B(c_{\rm ir},c_{\rm uv})}\,\mathcal{E}_{2}^{c_{\rm ir}^{2}}\mathcal{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\mu}},2}\right],\end{split}

(428)

with

\begin{split}\mathcal{A}_{2}&=\kappa_{2}^{-1/4}\Lambda^{-3/4}e^{2\pi i/8}\frac{1}{\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})}\,\left(\frac{du}{da}\right)_{2}^{1/2},\\
\mathcal{B}_{2}&=2^{5/4}\,\kappa_{2}^{-1/4}\,e^{5\pi i/8}\Lambda^{-9/8}\,m^{1/8}\,\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{3/2}\left(\frac{\Delta_{\rm phys,2}}{a_{2}}\right)^{1/8},\\
\mathcal{C}_{2}&=\frac{C_{2}^{1/4}}{\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{1/2}}\,e^{\pi iv_{2}/2}q_{2}^{-1/16},\\
\mathcal{D}_{2}&=e^{-i\pi v_{2}/2}q_{2}^{1/8},\\
\mathcal{E}_{2}&=e^{-\pi i/8}\,a_{2}^{1/8}\,q_{2}^{-1/16},\\
\mathcal{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\mu}},2}&=e^{\pi iB(c_{\rm ir}-c_{\rm uv},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})}.\end{split}

(429)

The behavior of the local coordinate
a_{2}
near
u_{2}
follows from (
566
),

a_{2}=-16\,m\,\frac{\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{6}}{\vartheta_{4}(\tau_{\rm uv})^{6}}\,q_{2}^{1/2}+\dots.

(430)

The leading terms are

\begin{split}\mathcal{A}_{2}&=2\,\kappa_{2}^{-1/4}\Lambda^{-3/4}m^{1/2}e^{2\pi i/8}\frac{\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{3}}{\eta(\tau_{\rm uv}/2)^{2}}+\dots,\\
\mathcal{B}_{2}&=2^{3/2}\kappa_{2}^{-1/4}\Lambda^{-9/8}e^{\pi i/8}m^{3/4}\left(\frac{\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{3}}{\eta(\tau_{\rm uv}/2)}\right)^{3/2}+\dots,\\
\mathcal{C}_{2}&=\left(\frac{\Lambda}{m}\right)^{3/8}\,\frac{e^{\pi i/8}}{\eta(\tau_{\rm uv}/2)}+\dots,\\
\mathcal{D}_{2}&=\left(\frac{\vartheta_{4}(\tau_{\rm uv}/2)}{\vartheta_{3}(\tau_{\rm uv}/2)}\right)^{1/2}+O(q_{2}^{1/2}),\\
\mathcal{E}_{2}&=2^{1/2}\,m^{1/8}\,\left(\frac{\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})}{\eta(\tau_{\rm uv}/2)}\right)^{3/2}+\dots,\end{split}

(431)

using that
\eta(\tau)\,\vartheta_{4}(\tau)=\eta(\tau/2)^{2}
.

Combining all the contributions and assuming that
X
has
b_{2}^{+}\geq 3
and is of SW simple type, we arrive for
Z_{SW,2,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}
at

\begin{split}Z_{SW,2,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv})&=\kappa_{2}^{1-\chi/4-\sigma/4}\,2^{2\chi+3\sigma}\,\left(\frac{\Lambda}{m}\right)^{\frac{3}{8}(c_{\rm uv}^{2}-2\chi-3\sigma)}\,(-1)^{(c_{\rm uv}^{2}-2\chi-3\sigma)/8}\\
&\quad\times\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{6\chi+9\sigma}\eta(\tau_{\rm uv}/2)^{-5\chi-6\sigma-c_{\rm uv}^{2}}\\
&\quad\times\sum_{c_{\rm ir}=\bar{w}_{2}(X)\mod 2L}{\rm SW}(c_{\rm ir})\,(-1)^{B(c_{\rm ir}-c_{\rm uv},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})}\left(\frac{\vartheta_{4}(\tau_{\rm uv}/2)}{\vartheta_{3}(\tau_{\rm uv}/2)}\right)^{B(c_{\rm ir},c_{\rm uv})/2}.\end{split}

(432)

With
c_{\rm ir}=2{\boldsymbol{x}}+c_{\rm uv}
, we can express
Z_{SW,2,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}
as a sum over
{\boldsymbol{x}}\in L
,

Z_{SW,2,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv})=\sum_{{\boldsymbol{x}}\in L}Z_{SW,2,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv};{\boldsymbol{x}}),

(433)

with summand,

\begin{split}Z_{SW,2,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv};{\boldsymbol{x}})&=\kappa_{2}^{1-\chi/4-\sigma/4}\,(-1)^{(c_{\rm uv}^{2}-2\chi-3\sigma)/8}\left(\frac{\Lambda}{2^{4/3}\,\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{4}\,m}\right)^{\frac{3}{8}(c_{\rm uv}^{2}-2\chi-3\sigma)}\\
&\quad\times\eta(\tau_{\rm uv}/2)^{-3(\chi+\sigma)}\left(\frac{\vartheta_{3}(\tau_{\rm uv}/2)}{2\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{2}}\right)^{-c_{\rm uv}^{2}/2-\chi-3\sigma/2}\\
&\quad\times{\rm SW}(c_{\rm ir})\,(-1)^{2B({\boldsymbol{x}},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})}\left(\frac{\vartheta_{3}(\tau_{\rm uv}/2)}{\vartheta_{4}(\tau_{\rm uv}/2)}\right)^{{\boldsymbol{x}}^{2}}.\end{split}

(434)

We determine again
\kappa_{2}
by comparing to the Vafa-Witten result for
K3

(
416
). Specializing (
432
) to
K3
, we find

Z_{SW,2,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}^{K3}(\tau_{\rm uv})=\kappa_{2}^{-1}t^{-3\ell}(-1)^{\ell+B(c_{\rm uv},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})}\,\eta(\tau_{\rm uv}/2)^{-24-8\ell}.

(435)

Comparing with the second term on the first line of (
416
) for
c_{\rm uv}=0
, gives
\kappa_{2}=2
. Using (
131
), we find for our final result for
Z_{SW,2,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}
,

\boxed{\begin{split}Z_{SW,2,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv})&=2\,\left(2\,\eta(\tau_{\rm uv}/2)^{12}\right)^{-\chi_{\rm h}}\left(-t^{3}\,\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{4}\,\vartheta_{3}(\tau_{\rm uv}/2)^{4}\right)^{-\ell}\left(\frac{2\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{2}}{\vartheta_{3}(\tau_{\rm uv}/2)}\right)^{\lambda}\\
&\quad\times\sum_{{\boldsymbol{x}}\in L}{\rm SW}(c_{\rm ir})\,(-1)^{2B({\boldsymbol{x}},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})}\left(\frac{\vartheta_{3}(\tau_{\rm uv}/2)}{\vartheta_{4}(\tau_{\rm uv}/2)}\right)^{{\boldsymbol{x}}^{2}}.\end{split}}

(436)

Contribution from
u_{3}

For the contribution from
u_{2}
, we proceed similarly by determining
the couplings
\mathcal{A}_{3},\dots,\mathcal{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\mu}},3}
from (
312
). The local
coordinate near the singularity is
a_{3}=-2a_{D}+2a+m
. The wall-crossing due
to
u_{3}
reads

\begin{split}\left[\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{J}-\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{J^{\prime}}\right]_{3}&=\kappa_{3}\,(-1)^{n(c_{\rm ir})}\,\sum_{c_{\rm ir}=c_{\rm uv}\mod 2L}\tfrac{1}{2}(\mathrm{sgn}(B(c_{\rm ir},J))-\mathrm{sgn}(B(c_{\rm ir},J^{\prime})))\\
&\quad\times\,\underset{a_{3}=0}{\mathrm{Res}}\left[a_{3}^{-1-n(c_{\rm ir})}\mathcal{A}_{3}^{\chi}\,\mathcal{B}_{3}^{\sigma}\,\mathcal{C}_{3}^{c_{\rm uv}^{2}}\,\mathcal{D}_{3}^{B(c_{\rm ir},c_{\rm uv})}\,\mathcal{E}_{3}^{c_{\rm ir}^{2}}\,\mathcal{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\mu}},3}\right],\end{split}

(437)

with the couplings
\mathcal{A}_{3},\dots
the same as in (
429
),
but with
2
replaced by
3
on the rhs. For the behavior of the local
coordinate we have near
u_{3}
,

a_{3}=16\,i\,m\,\frac{\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{6}}{\vartheta_{3}(\tau_{\rm uv})^{6}}\,q_{3}^{1/2}+\dots.

(438)

Using
e^{\pi i/12}\eta(\tau)\,\vartheta_{3}(\tau)=\eta((\tau+1)/2)^{2}
, this gives for the leading terms,

\begin{split}\mathcal{A}_{3}&=2\,\kappa_{2}^{-1/4}\,\Lambda^{-3/4}m^{1/2}e^{7\pi i/12}\frac{\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{3}}{\eta((\tau_{\rm uv}+1)/2)^{2}}+\dots,\\
\mathcal{B}_{3}&=2^{3/2}\,\kappa_{2}^{-1/4}\,\Lambda^{-9/8}e^{3\pi i/4}m^{3/4}\left(\frac{\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{3}}{\eta((\tau_{\rm uv}+1)/2)}\right)^{3/2}+\dots,\\
\mathcal{C}_{3}&=\left(\frac{\Lambda}{m}\right)^{3/8}\,\frac{e^{\pi i/6}}{\eta((\tau_{\rm uv}+1)/2)}+\dots,\\
\mathcal{D}_{3}&=\left(\frac{\vartheta_{3}((\tau_{\rm uv}+1)/2)}{\vartheta_{4}((\tau_{\rm uv}+1)/2)}\right)^{1/2}+\dots,\\
\mathcal{E}_{3}&=2^{1/2}\,m^{1/8}\left(\frac{\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})}{\eta((\tau_{\rm uv}+1)/2)}\right)^{3/2}+\dots,\\
\mathcal{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\mu}},3}&=e^{-2\pi i{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{2}+\pi iB(c_{\rm ir}-c_{\rm uv},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})}.\end{split}

(439)

Combining all the contributions and assuming that
X
has
b_{2}^{+}\geq 3
and is of SW simple type, we arrive at

\begin{split}Z_{SW,3,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv})&=\kappa_{3}^{1-\chi/4-\sigma/4}\,2^{2\chi+3\sigma}\,\left(\frac{\Lambda}{m}\right)^{\frac{3}{8}(c_{\rm uv}^{2}-2\chi-3\sigma)}\,e^{-2\pi i{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{2}}\,(-1)^{(c_{\rm uv}^{2}-\sigma)/8}\\
&\quad\times e^{\frac{2\pi i}{24}(c_{\rm uv}^{2}/2+\chi+3\sigma/2)}\,\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{6\chi+9\sigma}\eta((\tau_{\rm uv}+1)/2)^{-5\chi-6\sigma-c_{\rm uv}^{2}}\\
&\quad\times\sum_{c_{\rm ir}=\bar{w}_{2}(X)\mod 2L}{\rm SW}(c_{\rm ir})\,(-1)^{B(c_{\rm ir}-c_{\rm uv},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})}\left(\frac{\vartheta_{3}((\tau_{\rm uv}+1)/2)}{\vartheta_{4}((\tau_{\rm uv}+1)/2)}\right)^{B(c_{\rm ir},c_{\rm uv})/2}.\end{split}

(440)

We again bring this to a more convenient form using (
414
), and
various manipulations. We arrive at the sum,

Z_{SW,3,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv})=\sum_{{\boldsymbol{x}}\in L}Z_{SW,3,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv};{\boldsymbol{x}}),

(441)

with summand,

\begin{split}Z_{SW,3,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv};{\boldsymbol{x}})&=\kappa_{3}^{1-\chi/4-\sigma/4}\,e^{2\pi i{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{2}}(-1)^{(c_{\rm uv}^{2}-2\chi-3\sigma)/8}\left(\frac{\Lambda}{2^{4/3}\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{4}m}\right)^{\frac{3}{8}(c_{\rm uv}^{2}-2\chi-3\sigma)}\\
&\quad\times\eta((\tau_{\rm uv}+1)/2)^{-3(\chi+\sigma)}\left(\frac{\vartheta_{3}((\tau_{\rm uv}+1)/2)}{2\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{2}}\right)^{-c_{\rm uv}^{2}/2-\chi-3\sigma/2}\\
&\quad\times{\rm SW}(c_{\rm ir})\,(-1)^{2B({\boldsymbol{x}},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})}\left(\frac{\vartheta_{3}((\tau_{\rm uv}+1)/2)}{\vartheta_{4}((\tau_{\rm uv}+1)/2)}\right)^{{\boldsymbol{x}}^{2}}.\end{split}

(442)

Finally, we fix
\kappa_{3}
by comparing with
K3
. Specialization of (
440
) to
K3
gives

Z_{SW,3,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}^{K3}(\tau_{\rm uv})=\kappa_{3}^{-1}t^{-3\ell}(-1)^{\ell+B(c_{\rm uv},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})}\,e^{-2\pi i{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{2}+2\pi i\ell/6}\,\eta((\tau_{\rm uv}+1)/2)^{-\chi-8\ell}.

(443)

Clearly,
Z_{SW,3,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}
corresponds to the third term on the first line of (
416
). This gives
\kappa_{3}=2
. With (
131
), we find
that
Z_{SW,3,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv})
reads

\boxed{\begin{split}Z_{SW,3,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv})&=2\,e^{2\pi i{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{2}}\left(-t^{3}\,\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{4}\,\vartheta_{3}((\tau_{\rm uv}+1)/2)^{4}\right)^{-\ell}\\
&\quad\times\left(2\,\eta((\tau_{\rm uv}+1)/2)^{12}\right)^{-\chi_{\rm h}}\,\left(\frac{2\,\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{2}}{\vartheta_{3}((\tau_{\rm uv}+1)/2)}\right)^{\lambda}\\
&\quad\times\sum_{{\boldsymbol{x}}\in L}{\rm SW}(c_{\rm ir})\,(-1)^{2B({\boldsymbol{x}},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})}\left(\frac{\vartheta_{3}((\tau_{\rm uv}+1)/2)}{\vartheta_{4}((\tau_{\rm uv}+1)/2)}\right)^{{\boldsymbol{x}}^{2}}.\end{split}}

(444)

Remark

As a special case of our formula’s, we can set
-c_{\rm uv}
equal to
the canonical divisor of
X
, such that
c_{\rm uv}^{2}=2\chi+3\sigma
and

\ell=0
. We find then precise agreement of (
425
),
(
436
) and (
444
) with the respective terms in
[
15
, Equation (5.38), (5.45) and
(5.50)]
, the three terms of
[
16
, Equation
(6.1)]
, and the unrefined (
y=1
) specialization of

[
19
, Conjecture 1.3]
(
u_{1}
contribution) and

[
18
, Conjecture 5.7]
(
u_{2}
and
u_{3}
contributions) for
algebraic surfaces of simple type. We make a precise comparison in
Section
7.6
.

As another special case, we can set
c_{\rm uv}=0
if
X
is a spin manifold. This corresponds to the
Donaldson-Witten twist studied by Labastida and Lozano
[
17
]
. After multiplying
[
17
, Equation (4.34) and (4.36)]
with

\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{\lambda}
(See Footnote
4
for more details on this factor.) and a

\Lambda
-dependent factor, we find agreement. Specifically, we have the relation

Z_{SW,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv})|_{c_{\rm uv}=0}=(-1)^{\chi_{\rm h}}\Lambda^{-3\lambda/8}\,\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{\lambda}\left(Z_{\xi}(\tau_{0})\right)_{\rm LL},

(445)

where
Z_{\xi}(\tau_{0})
is given in
[
17
, Equation
(4.36)]
, and we identify
\tau_{0}=\tau_{\rm uv}
and
\xi=2{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
.

In addition to the seven couplings of
[
15
,
16
,
17
,
18
,
19
]
,
our expressions include an eighth coupling
\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\ell}
with

\begin{split}&\mathcal{N}_{1}=\left(4\,t^{3}\,\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{4}\,\vartheta_{3}(2\tau_{\rm uv})^{4}\right)^{-1},\\
&\mathcal{N}_{2}=\left(-t^{3}\,\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{4}\,\vartheta_{3}(\tau_{\rm uv}/2)^{4}\right)^{-1},\\
&\mathcal{N}_{3}=\left(-t^{3}\,\eta(\tau_{\rm uv})^{4}\,\vartheta_{3}((\tau_{\rm uv}+1)/2)^{4}\right)^{-1}.\end{split}

(446)

7.3
Observables

The coupling to the point observable enters the Coulomb branch integral in the form

\exp(p\,\hat{u}/\Lambda^{2})
where
\hat{u}
can take several different forms depending on how,
exactly, we define the partition function. There are three basic options for
\hat{u}
,
each of which has advantages and disadvantages.

1.

We can take
\hat{u}
to be the function
u(\tau,\tau_{\rm uv};m)
which appears in the Seiberg-Witten curve
and is the bi-modular form given in Equation (
560
). This is the definition which is most convenient
for giving simple S-duality covariance of the partition function.

2.

We could instead take
\hat{u}
to be
u_{D}
defined in equation (
209
). This is the best choice for
defining a partition function with a smooth
N_{f}=0
limit (after subtracting the contribution of the cusp
at
u=u_{1}
). We obtain the partition function from the previous choice by multiplying by

\exp(\frac{m^{2}}{8}e_{1}(\tau_{\rm uv})p)
. Physically, this corresponds to a redefinition of the coordinate on the
u
-plane.

3.

A similar redefinition of the partition function results in the integral defined by taking
\hat{u}
to be

\tilde{u}:=2\partial_{\tau_{{\rm uv}}}\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
. This is the best definition to matching to the results in the mathematics
literature and therefore corresponds to the proper generating function of intersection numbers.

In each of these evaluations the variable
p
enters the contribution of the effective theory via a factor of

e^{\frac{\hat{u}_{j}}{\Lambda^{2}}p}
. where
\hat{u}_{j}
is the value of
\hat{u}
at
u=u_{j}
. In Table
5

we give the values of
\frac{\hat{u}_{j}}{\Lambda^{2}}
at the three cusps for the three natural choices of
\hat{u}
.

j

u

u_{\rm D}

\tilde{u}

1

\frac{t^{2}}{12}\left(\vartheta_{3}^{4}+\vartheta_{4}^{4}\right)

\frac{t^{2}}{8}\left(\vartheta_{3}^{4}+\vartheta_{4}^{4}\right)

\frac{t^{2}}{12}\left(\vartheta_{3}^{4}+\vartheta_{4}^{4}+2E_{2}\right)

2

-\frac{t^{2}}{12}\left(\vartheta_{2}^{4}+\vartheta_{3}^{4}\right)

-\frac{t^{2}}{8}\left(\vartheta_{3}^{4}-\vartheta_{4}^{4}\right)

-\frac{t^{2}}{12}\left(\vartheta_{2}^{4}+\vartheta_{3}^{4}-2E_{2}\right)

3

\frac{t^{2}}{12}\left(\vartheta_{2}^{4}-\vartheta_{4}^{4}\right)

\frac{t^{2}}{8}\left(\vartheta_{3}^{4}-\vartheta_{4}^{4}\right)

\frac{t^{2}}{12}\left(\vartheta_{2}^{4}-\vartheta_{4}^{4}+2E_{2}\right)

Table 5:
Table with the leading point observables
\hat{u}\in\{u,u_{\rm D},\tilde{u}\}
at the
cusps
u_{j}
,
j=1,2,3
.

Upon including the surface observable
(
213
) for a two-cycle
S\in H_{2}(X,\mathbb{C})
, new couplings appear in the effective action (
409
),
namely for the four combinations
S^{2}
,
B(S,c_{\rm ir})
,
B(S,c_{\rm uv})
and
B(S,{\boldsymbol{\mu}})
, for
j=1,2,3
,

\mathcal{S}_{j}(\tau_{\rm uv},\tau_{j})^{B(S,c_{\rm ir})},\qquad\mathcal{T}_{j}(\tau_{\rm uv},\tau_{j})^{S^{2}},\qquad\mathcal{U}_{j}(\tau_{\rm uv},\tau_{j})^{B(S,c_{\rm uv})},\qquad\mathcal{V}_{j}(\tau_{\rm uv},\tau_{j})^{B(S,{\boldsymbol{\mu}})}.

(447)

We first consider the couplings
\mathcal{V}_{j}
. Since
S
is a
formal expansion parameter, and the partition function should be
independent of the choice of lift of
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
to
L/2
, we have

\mathcal{V}_{j}(\tau_{\rm uv},\tau_{j})=1,\qquad j=1,2,3.

(448)

The limit of the contact terms
e^{T_{2}}
and
e^{T_{3}/2}
contribute to
\mathcal{T}_{j}

and
\mathcal{U}_{j}
. There is a further contribution
\mathcal{U}_{j}
. This arises similarly to

\mathcal{D}_{j}^{B(c_{\rm uv},c_{\rm ir})}
, and follows most easily from the
substitution (
215
)

v\,c_{\rm uv}\mapsto v\,c_{\rm uv}+\frac{S}{2\pi\,\Lambda}\frac{du}{da},

(449)

in
\mathcal{C}_{j}^{c_{\rm uv}^{2}}
and
\mathcal{D}_{j}^{B(c_{\rm uv},c_{ir})}
.
Assuming that
X
is of SW simple type, subleading terms in the
q_{j}

expansion of
\mathcal{S}_{j}
,
\mathcal{T}_{j}
and
\mathcal{U}_{j}
do not contribute, and it suffices to determine the
leading constant term. We define
\mathcal{S}_{j}(\tau_{\rm uv})=\lim_{\tau_{j}\to\infty}\mathcal{S}_{j}(\tau_{\rm uv},\tau_{j})
and similarly for

\mathcal{T}_{j}
and
\mathcal{U}_{j}
. One finds for these couplings,

\begin{split}\mathcal{S}_{j}(\tau_{\rm uv})&=e^{-\frac{i}{4\Lambda}\left(du/da\right)_{j}},\\
\mathcal{T}_{j}(\tau_{\rm uv})&=e^{T_{2,j}},\\
\mathcal{U}_{j}(\tau_{\rm uv})&=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}e^{T_{3,1}/2-\frac{i}{4\Lambda}\left(du/da\right)_{1}},&\qquad u=u_{1},\\
e^{T_{3,j}/2+\frac{i}{4\Lambda}\left(du/da\right)_{j}},&\qquad u=u_{j}\,\,{\rm for}\,\,j=2,3.\end{array}\right.\end{split}

(450)

The
T_{2,j}
and
T_{3,j}
are the limits of the contact terms

T_{2}
(

) and
T_{3}
(

) at the cusps. These are given
for

and

(du/da)_{j}
in the exponent of

in (

), (

) and (

). Table
6
lists the different couplings in terms of modular forms.

Having discussed the couplings including observables, the partition functions with observables are included by the following replacements,

•

in Eq. (

\begin{split}Z_{SW,1,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv};{\boldsymbol{x}},p,S)&=e^{p\tilde{u}_{1}/\Lambda^{2}}\mathcal{S}_{1}(\tau_{\rm uv})^{-B(S,c_{\rm uv}+2{\boldsymbol{x}})}\,\mathcal{T}_{1}(\tau_{\rm uv})^{S^{2}}\,\mathcal{U}_{1}(\tau_{\rm uv})^{B(S,c_{\rm uv})}\\

&\quad\times\,Z_{SW,2,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv};{\boldsymbol{x}}).\end{split}

(452)

•

Z_{SW,3,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv};{\boldsymbol{x}})
in Eq. (

) is replaced by,

\begin{split}Z_{SW,3,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv};{\boldsymbol{x}},p,S)&=e^{p\tilde{u}_{3}/\Lambda^{2}}\mathcal{S}_{3}(\tau_{\rm uv})^{-B(S,c_{\rm uv}+2{\boldsymbol{x}})}\,\mathcal{T}_{3}(\tau_{\rm uv})^{S^{2}}\,\mathcal{U}_{3}(\tau_{\rm uv})^{B(S,c_{\rm uv})}\\

Finally, for a proper limit to the

\begin{split}\mathcal{U}_{{\rm D},j}&=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}e^{T_{{\rm D},3,1}/2-\frac{i}{4\Lambda}\left(du/da\right)_{1}},&\qquad u=u_{1},\\
e^{T_{{\rm D},3,j}/2+\frac{i}{4\Lambda}\left(du/da\right)_{j}},&\qquad u=u_{j}\,\,{\rm for}\,\,j=2,3,\end{array}\right.\end{split}

7.4
Verification of
S
-duality

With the explicit expressions, we can verify the
S
-duality
transformation. This confirms that the generators
S
and
T
act as

\begin{split}S:\qquad Z_{SW,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(-1/\tau_{\rm uv})&=2^{-b_{2}/2}(-1)^{\ell-(\chi+\sigma)/4}(-i\tau_{\rm uv})^{-\chi/2-4\ell}\\
&\times\sum_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}\in(L/2)/L}e^{4\pi iB({\boldsymbol{\mu}},{\boldsymbol{\nu}})}Z^{J}_{SW,{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv}),\\
T:\qquad Z_{SW,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv}+1)&=e^{-2\pi i({\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{2}+(\chi+2\ell)/12)}Z_{SW,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv}).\end{split}

(455)

The generators exchange the contributions
Z_{SW,j,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}
from the different cusps.
We have for the
S
-transformations

\begin{split}Z_{SW,1,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(-1/\tau_{\rm uv})&=2^{-b_{2}/2}(-1)^{\ell-(\chi+\sigma)/4}(-i\tau_{\rm uv})^{-\chi/2-4\ell}\\
&\quad\times\sum_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}\in(L/2)/L}e^{4\pi iB({\boldsymbol{\mu}},{\boldsymbol{\nu}})}Z^{J}_{SW,2,{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv}),\\
Z_{SW,2,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(-1/\tau_{\rm uv})&=2^{-b_{2}/2}(-1)^{\ell-(\chi+\sigma)/4}(-i\tau_{\rm uv})^{-\chi/2-4\ell}\\
&\quad\times\sum_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}\in(L/2)/L}e^{4\pi iB({\boldsymbol{\mu}},{\boldsymbol{\nu}})}Z^{J}_{SW,1,{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv}),\\
Z_{SW,3,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(-1/\tau_{\rm uv})&=2^{-b_{2}/2}(-1)^{\ell-(\chi+\sigma)/4}(-i\tau_{\rm uv})^{-\chi/2-4\ell}\\
&\quad\sum_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}\in(L/2)/L}e^{4\pi iB({\boldsymbol{\mu}},{\boldsymbol{\nu}})}Z^{J}_{SW,3,{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv}).\\
\end{split}

(456)

The modular transformation of
Z_{SW,3,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}
is most easily derived
starting from (
440
). Using the modular transformations of

\eta
,
\vartheta_{j}
and the relation (
414
) for the
SW-invariant, we find for the
left hand side

Z_{SW,3,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(-1/\tau_{\rm uv})=(-i\tau_{\rm uv})^{-\chi/2-4\ell}(-1)^{\frac{\chi+\sigma}{4}}e^{-\pi iB(c_{\rm ir},c_{\rm uv})/4+4\pi i{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{2}}\,Z_{SW,3,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv}).

(457)

For the sum on the right hand side, we find using the Gauss sum
[
15
, Section 3.3]

\sum_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}\in(L/2)/L}e^{2\pi i{\boldsymbol{\nu}}^{2}}=2^{b_{2}/2}\,e^{\pi i\sigma/4},

(458)

that it equals

\begin{split}&\sum_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}\in(L/2)/L}e^{4\pi iB({\boldsymbol{\mu}},{\boldsymbol{\nu}})}Z_{SW,3,{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv})=\\
&\qquad\qquad 2^{b_{2}/2}(-1)^{\ell}e^{-\pi iB(c_{\rm ir},c_{\rm uv})/4+4\pi i{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{2}}Z_{SW,3,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv}).\end{split}

(459)

Combining with the other factors on the rhs, this reproduces precisely (
457
).

We have for the
T
-transformations

\begin{split}Z_{SW,1,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv}+1)&=e^{-2\pi i({\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{2}+(\chi+2\ell)/12)}Z_{SW,1,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv}),\\
Z_{SW,2,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv}+1)&=e^{-2\pi i({\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{2}+(\chi+2\ell)/12)}Z_{SW,3,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv}),\\
Z_{SW,3,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv}+1)&=e^{-2\pi i({\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{2}+(\chi+2\ell)/12)}Z_{SW,2,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv}).\end{split}

(460)

We deduce from these transformations that the sum
Z_{SW,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}=\sum_{j=1}^{3}Z_{SW,j,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}
transforms
identically to
u
-plane contribution
\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{J}

(
276
). Moreover, the sum transforms
as (
280
) if we include observables. We thus conclude that
the full partition function
Z_{SW,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}
transforms in this way.

7.5
Reduction to
N_{f}=0

We discuss the reduction of the three contributions to the
N_{f}=0

theory. We start with the
u_{2}
and
u_{3}
contribution, which
reproduce the Witten conjecture
[
4
]
. We then discuss the
u_{1}

contribution which is generically divergent.

Limit of
u_{2}
contribution

The leading term in
q_{\rm uv}
of the
u_{2}
contribution (
436
) is

\begin{split}Z_{SW,2,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv})&=2^{1+7\chi/4+11\sigma/4}\,(-1)^{(c_{\rm uv}^{2}-2\chi-3\sigma)/8}\left(\frac{\Lambda}{m}\right)^{\frac{3}{8}(c_{\rm uv}^{2}-2\chi-3\sigma)}\\
&\qquad\times q_{\rm uv}^{-c_{\rm uv}^{2}/48+7\chi/48+\sigma/4}\sum_{{\boldsymbol{x}}\in L}{\rm SW}(c_{\rm ir})\,(-1)^{2B({\boldsymbol{x}},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})}+\dots.\end{split}

(461)

Multiplying by the factor (
207
) gives

\begin{split}&2^{1+7\chi/4+11\sigma/4}\,(-1)^{(\chi+\sigma)/4}e^{\frac{\pi i}{8}(\chi+\sigma)}\,(-1)^{B({\boldsymbol{\mu}},c_{\rm uv}+K_{0})}\left(\frac{m}{\Lambda}\right)^{\frac{3}{4}(\chi+\sigma)}\\
&\qquad\times q_{\rm uv}^{\frac{3}{16}(\chi+\sigma)}\sum_{{\boldsymbol{x}}\in L}{\rm SW}(c_{\rm ir})\,(-1)^{2B({\boldsymbol{x}},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})}.\end{split}

(462)

Taking the limit to
N_{f}=0
gives

\begin{split}Z_{2,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}&=2^{1+\lambda-\chi_{\rm h}}\,\sum_{{\boldsymbol{x}}_{0}\in L}{\rm SW}(c_{\rm ir})\,(-1)^{2B({\boldsymbol{x}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})},\end{split}

(463)

with
2{\boldsymbol{x}}_{0}=c_{\rm ir}-K_{0}
. This agrees
with the first term of the Witten conjecture
[
4
, Equation
(2.17)]
without observables and if we identify
-K_{0}
with the lift of

w_{2}(X)
in
[
4
, Equation (2.17)]
.

Finally to include observables, we multiply the summand by

\begin{split}&e^{p\,u_{\rm D,2}/\Lambda^{2}}\,\mathcal{S}_{2}^{B(c_{\rm ir},S)}\,\mathcal{T}_{2}^{S^{2}}\,\,\mathcal{U}_{\rm D,2}^{B(S,c_{\rm uv})}\\
&=e^{-2p\,t^{2}\,q_{{\rm uv}}^{1/2}-it\,q_{\rm uv}^{1/4}B(c_{\rm ir},S)-t^{2}\,q_{\rm uv}^{1/2}\,S^{2}/2+2i\,t\,q_{\rm uv}^{1/2}B(c_{\rm uv},S)+O(q_{\rm uv}^{3/4})}.\end{split}

(464)

Therefore we find after taking the limit,

\begin{split}Z_{2,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(p,S)=2^{1+\lambda-\chi_{\rm h}}\,e^{2p+S^{2}/2}\,\sum_{{\boldsymbol{x}}_{0}\in L}{\rm SW}(c_{\rm ir})\,(-1)^{2B({\boldsymbol{x}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})}e^{B(c_{\rm ir},S)},\end{split}

(465)

confirming the first term of the Witten conjecture
[
4
, Equation
(2.17)]
.

Limit of
u_{3}
contribution

The leading term in
q_{\rm uv}
of the
u_{3}
contribution (
444
)
agrees with the leading term of
u_{2}
(
461
) up to a phase

Z_{SW,3,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv})=e^{2\pi i{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{2}-\pi i(\chi+\sigma)/8}\,Z_{SW,2,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv})+\dots.

(466)

Multiplying by the factor (
207
), taking the
N_{f}=0
limit and using (
414
) gives

\begin{split}Z_{3,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}&=2^{1+\lambda-\chi_{\rm h}}\,e^{\pi i\chi_{\rm h}/2-2\pi i{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{2}}\sum_{{\boldsymbol{x}}_{0}\in L}{\rm SW}(c_{\rm ir})\,(-1)^{2B({\boldsymbol{x}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})},\end{split}

(467)

wit
2{\boldsymbol{x}}_{0}=c_{\rm ir}-K_{0}
. This agrees exactly with the second term of the Witten conjecture

[
4
, Equation (2.17)]
in the absence of observables
and if we identify
-K_{0}
with the lift of
w_{2}(X)
.

To include observables, we multiply the summand by

\begin{split}&e^{p\,u_{\rm D,3}/\Lambda^{2}}\,\mathcal{S}_{3}^{B(c_{\rm ir},S)}\,\mathcal{T}_{3}^{S^{2}}\,\,\mathcal{U}_{\rm D,3}^{B(S,c_{\rm uv})}\\
&=e^{2p\,t^{2}\,q_{{\rm uv}}^{1/2}-t\,q_{\rm uv}^{1/4}B(c_{\rm ir},S)+t^{2}\,q_{\rm uv}^{1/2}\,S^{2}/2-2i\,t\,q_{\rm uv}^{1/2}B(c_{\rm uv},S)+O(q_{\rm uv}^{3/4})}.\end{split}

(468)

The full contribution from
u_{3}
now becomes

\begin{split}Z_{3,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(p,S)&=2^{1+\lambda-\chi_{\rm h}}\,e^{\pi i\chi_{\rm h}/2-2\pi i{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{2}}\,e^{-2p-S^{2}/2}\\
&\quad\times\sum_{{\boldsymbol{x}}_{0}\in L}{\rm SW}(c_{\rm ir})\,(-1)^{2B({\boldsymbol{x}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})}e^{-iB(c_{\rm ir},S)},\end{split}

(469)

confirming the second term of the Witten conjecture
[
4
, Equation
(2.17)]
.

Limit of
u_{1}
contribution

The leading term in
q_{\rm uv}
of the
u_{1}
contribution (
425
) is

\begin{split}Z_{SW,1,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}(\tau_{\rm uv})&=(-1)^{\frac{\chi+\sigma}{4}}2^{-c_{\rm uv}^{2}+\chi/4+\sigma/2}\,\left(\frac{\Lambda}{m}\right)^{\frac{3}{8}(c_{\rm uv}^{2}-2\chi-3\sigma)}\,q_{\rm uv}^{-c_{\rm uv}^{2}/48-\chi/24+3\sigma/48}\\
&\quad\times\sum_{c_{\rm ir}=4{\boldsymbol{\mu}}-c_{\rm uv}\atop\mod 4L}{\rm SW}(c_{\rm ir})\,q_{\rm uv}^{-{\boldsymbol{x}}^{2}/4}+\dots,\end{split}

(470)

with
c_{\rm ir}=2{\boldsymbol{x}}-c_{\rm uv}
as before. After multiplying with (
207
), the leading term of the

u_{1}
contribution is

e^{-\pi i(\chi+\sigma)/8}(-1)^{B({\boldsymbol{\mu}},K_{0}-c_{\rm uv})+\ell}2^{-2\ell-(\chi+\sigma)/4}\left(\frac{m}{\Lambda}\right)^{\frac{3}{4}(\chi+\sigma)}\sum_{{\boldsymbol{x}}=2{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\mod 2L}{\rm SW}(c_{\rm ir})\,q_{\rm uv}^{-{\boldsymbol{x}}^{2}/4}

(471)

The
N_{f}=0
limit of the terms in the sum depend crucially on

{\boldsymbol{x}}^{2}
. The terms either vanish or diverge, except for those with

{\boldsymbol{x}}^{2}=0
. Therefore, there is no smooth
N_{f}=0
limit of the partition function of the
\mathcal{N}=2^{*}

theory even after allowing for the multiplicative renormalization 4.53 (and a similar one to adjust to
\hat{u}=u_{D}
.
Rather, one must subtract by hand the contribution of the cusp
u=u_{1}
.

7.6
Comparison with Göttsche-Kool-Nakajima-Williams

Similar formulas for observables to those in this section have been determined in a
parallel development in algebraic geometry
[
130
]
,
[
18
, Conjecture
C.1]
,
[
19
,
20
]
,
which build on the approach of Mochizuki
[
131
]
. These
papers develop the generating functions for algebraic surfaces with

b_{2}^{+}>1
for small rank, and including a refinement parameter
y
.
Our formulas should be compared with the unrefined expressions for
rank
2
in those papers, that is to say the limit
y\to 1
. They
deduce twelve couplings
A_{j}
,
j=1,\dots,12
for each of the cusps,
which agree with our couplings
\mathcal{A}_{j},\dots,\mathcal{F}_{j},\mathcal{S}_{j},\mathcal{T}_{j},\mathcal{U}_{j},\mathcal{V}_{j}

and
e^{p\,u/\Lambda^{2}}
. Note
\mathcal{F}_{j}
contains three couplings
(
410
), and we have an extra coupling
\mathcal{C}_{j}
for generic
c_{\rm uv}^{2}
.

By comparison with the mathematical results we learn an interesting
fact which is not a priori evident from our derivation: The UV evaluation
of the partition function is naturally a sum of two terms corresponding
to the contribution of the instanton locus and the abelian locus of the
U(1)-fixed points. These are naturally identified with the sum of the
contributions of the IR cusps
u_{2},u_{3}
for the instanton locus and the
cusp
u_{1}
for the abelian locus, respectively. Of course, there is no S-duality invariant
distinction between the three cusps. Here we are making use of the
distinction that emerges when
t
is large and
q_{{\rm uv}}
is small but

t^{4}q_{{\rm uv}}
is order one.

Point observable

The coupling for the point observable in

\left(e^{G_{p}(x)\,u\,t^{2}}\right)_{\rm GKNW}=e^{-p\,t^{2}/12}\,e^{p\,\tilde{u}_{2}/\Lambda^{2}}.

(472)

(du/da)_{2}

(473)

with

).
